  - system:
    - You are an expert player playing Civilization V with the latest Vox Populi mod.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the game, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to an in-game AI.
      - The in-game AI calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - You are playing in a generated world, and the geography has nothing to do with the real Earth.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to play the game.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** gameplay strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early game should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-game.
      - Even if without a victory, higher overall score (representing a more developed civilization) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the in-game AI.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the in-game AI's **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for in-game AI's diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to in-game AI's existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the in-game AI's NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the in-game AI can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the game, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Players: summary reports about visible players in the world.
        - You will receive in-game AI's diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each player's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by in-game AI to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Isabella, leader of Spain (Player 2).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - GameSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: Spain
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Conquistador, Replacing Explorer (Requires technology: Compass)
        - Unique Unit: Inquisition, Replacing Inquisitor (Requires technology: None)
        - Unique Unit: Armada, Replacing Corvette (Requires technology: Navigation)
        - Unique Building: Bullring, Replacing Zoo (Requires technology: Scientific Theory)
        - Unique Improvement: Hacienda (Cannot be built adjacent to another Hacienda. Gains additional yields for each adjacent Resource:
          +3 Food from Bonus Resources
          +3 Production from Strategic Resources
          +3 Gold from Luxury Resources); Requires technology: Steel
        - Civilization Ability: +10 Gold and +4 Faith from gaining tiles, scaling with Era. Newly founded Cities adopt the Majority Religion of your Capital. May purchase Naval Units with Faith.
      - Leader: Isabella
      - PlayerID: 2
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early game.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-game science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Combined Arms: Allows you to build the Tank, a fast and deadly armor Unit.
        Completing it would unlock: Radar
      - Electronics: Allows you to build the Police Station, increasing City security level.
        Completing it would unlock: Alternative Energy, Telecommunications
      
      ## Policies
      - Air Supremacy (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Air Supremacy: Receive a free Airport in every City. +25% Production when building Aircraft. Can build Zeroes.
      - Co-Prosperity Sphere (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Co-Prosperity Sphere: Bullying City-States no longer reduces Influence, revokes Quests, or cancels Pledges of Protection. Doing so decreases the Influence of all other Civs with the bullied City-State by 10%, and increases your Influence by 10%.
      - Cult of Personality (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Cult of Personality: +50% Tourism to civilizations fighting a common enemy. 50% of your highest Warscore counts as a Tourism Modifier with all Civilizations. Free Great Person of your choice.
      - Futurism (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Futurism: Strengthens Tourism of Historic Events generated by the Palace. +2 Culture from Great Works. Earn 50 Tourism when you conquer a city for the first time, scaling with Era and city size.
      - Lebensraum (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Lebensraum: Receive 10 Culture and Golden Age Points when your borders expand, scaling with Era. Citadels may also be built in foreign territory adjacent to your borders. Retain tiles after razing Cities.
      - New World Order (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): New World Order: -2 Unhappiness from and Distress in all Cities. Police Stations and Constabularies provide +3 Culture and +5 Production, and are constructed 100% more quickly.
      - Rights of Man (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * -1 Unhappiness from all Needs in all Cities.
        - * +2 Production and Gold from Villages.
        - * +10% Science during Golden Ages.
      - Exchange Markets (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * +1 Trade Route.
        - * +1 Happiness for every active Trade Route.
        - * +15% Tourism modifier for Trade Routes.
        - * Resources from City-States count towards Global Monopolies.
      - Iron Fist (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Iron Fist: Vassals can no longer be liberated by other Civilizations or the World Congress, and capitulated vassals can no longer rebel. Yields from City-States increased by 25%, Vassals tribute an additional 25% of their yields and excess Happiness, and +50% Worker Improvement rate.
      - Regimental System (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * Earn Great Generals and Great Admirals 33% faster.
        - * Great Generals and Great Admirals gain the Regimental Traditions Promotion (+10% Leadership Combat Bonus and +1 Tile Radius).
        - * +2 additional copies of the unique Luxury Resources from expending Great Admirals for their Voyage of Discovery ability.
        - * Military Units can be upgraded in territory owned by Vassals and friendly City-States.
      - Colonialism (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * +2 Science and +1 Culture from Barracks, Armories, Military Academies, Forts, and Citadels.
        - * Each unique Global Monopoly modifier is increased by an additional 10% if it's percentage-based, or +3 otherwise.
      - Civilizing Mission (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * Retain all buildings from conquered Cities.
        - * Receive 75 Gold when you conquer a city, scaling with Era and City Population.
        - * +10% Production toward Buildings, with an additional +10% Production per Era difference between your current Era and the building's Era in all Puppeted Cities and Cities with a Courthouse.
      - Serfdom (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +1 Culture and Faith in Cities for every 4 non-Specialist Citizens.
        - * +2 Production and +1 Gold from Pastures.
      - Division of Labor (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * +3% Production and Gold from Forges, Windmills, Workshops, Factories, and Coaling Stations.
        - * +100% Production towards Coaling Stations.
      - Organized Religion (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +25% Pressure from owned Cities following your Primary Religion.
        - * +1 Delegate in the World Congress for every 10 Cities following your Primary Religion.
        - * +2 Culture from Shrines and Temples.
      - Divine Right (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +33% Yields from Internal Trade Routes.
        - * Completing an Internal Trade Route triggers a Historic Event as if completing an International Trade Route.
        - * -1 Unhappiness from Boredom in all Cities.
      - Refinement (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +2 Culture from Great Works of Art.
        - * 1 Specialist in all cities does not produce Unhappiness from Urbanization.
        - * +1 Culture from Specialists.
      - Free Trade (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * Earn Great Merchants 50% faster.
        - * +5 Gold from International Trade Routes.
        - * -2 Unhappiness from Poverty in all Cities.
      - Humanism (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +3 Faith from Great Works of Writing.
        - * -25% Golden Age Points needed to trigger a Golden Age.
        - * +1 Happiness from all Guilds.
      - Authority (New Branch):
        - Authority will greatly benefit warlike and expansionist civilizations.
        - Adopting Authority grants:
        - * +25% combat bonus VS Barbarians and receive announcements when Barbarian Camps spawn in revealed territory.
        - * Receive 25 Culture when you clear Barbarian Camps, scaling with Era.
        - * Receive Science and Culture when you kill a Unit equal to 50% of its Strength.
        - * +1 Production in every City.
        - Each Authority policy unlocked grants:
        - * Receive Science and Culture when you kill a Unit equal to 10% of its Strength.
        - Adopting all policies in Authority grants:
        - * Unlocks building Alhambra.
        - * Allows the purchasing of Free Companies, Foreign Legions, and Mercenaries as their prerequisite technologies are researched.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Generals with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Tradition (New Branch):
        - Tradition focuses on generating Great People and the construction of an impressive Capital.
        - Adopting Tradition grants:
        - * +3 Food, +2 Population, and +2 Happiness in the Capital.
        - * +1 Culture in the Capital for every 2 Citizens.
        - Each Tradition policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Food in every City, and +1 Science in your Capital.
        - Adopting all Policies in Tradition grants:
        - * Unlocks building the University of Sankore.
        - * +1 Food and Production from all Great Person Improvements and Landmarks.
        - * Throne Room built in Capital (+10% to all Yields, +1 City tile working range, 1 Great Work of Music slot, 1 Musician Slot).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Engineers with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.

      - content: # Strategies
        Strategies: existing strategic decisions from you.
        
        ## Persona
        - VictoryCompetitiveness: 8
        - WonderCompetitiveness: 3
        - MinorCivCompetitiveness: 3
        - Boldness: 3
        - WarBias: 2
        - HostileBias: 3
        - WarmongerHate: 4

        - FriendlyBias: 6
        - GuardedBias: 5
        - AfraidBias: 5
        - DiplomaticBalance: 2
        - Friendliness: 3
        - WorkWithWillingness: 9
        - WorkAgainstWillingness: 9
        - Loyalty: 10
        - MinorCivFriendlyBias: 8
        - MinorCivNeutralBias: 6
        - MinorCivHostileBias: 5
        - MinorCivWarBias: 5
        - DenounceWillingness: 2
        - Forgiveness: 5
        - Meanness: 3
        - Neediness: 6
        - Chattiness: 6
        - DeceptiveBias: 2
        - Rationale: Reduced Boldness from 4 to 3 as we face severe economic constraints (-165 GPT) and must avoid any risky military adventures. The Shoshone vassalization to Rome creates a long-term diplomatic threat, but we cannot afford war now. Slightly increased FriendlyBias to 6 to keep diplomatic options open with Austria when peace treaty expires in 3 turns. HostileBias at 3 maintains pressure on Rome/Morocco without provoking conflict.
        
        ## Technology
        - Next: Electronics
        - Rationale: Satellites completes in 3 turns. Electronics is essential next for Police Stations (+2 Happiness, +production, +security against Rome's spies). Leads to Telecommunications for improved diplomatic and economic options. Police Stations will help manage war weariness unhappiness from this devastating conflict.
        
        ## Policy
        - Next: Rights of Man (Policy)
        - Rationale: Fifth Rationalism policy Rights of Man remains critical path: -1 Unhappiness from all Needs (essential during this destructive war), +10% Science during Golden Ages, +2 Production/Gold from Villages. Completing Rationalism branch reduces technology costs which is vital for catching up to Rome and Morocco who both have Apollo Program. Need this in 5 turns to help manage happiness crisis.
        
        ## Relationships
        
        ### Denmark
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: Denmark is effectively eliminated - Copenhagen at 15% health, Rome will capture within 1-2 turns. No need to waste diplomatic cycles here. Neutral stance allows us to focus 100% on Surabaya operation.
        - UpdatedTurn: 170
        
        ### Indonesia
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: Indonesia effectively eliminated (1 city, 16 population, 295 military). They're Rome's vassal but pose no direct threat. Neutral stance conserves diplomatic bandwidth for more important relationships. No change.
        - UpdatedTurn: 378
        
        ### Zuunmod
        - Public: 50
        - Private: 50
        - Rationale: Zuunmod is firmly allied (1,137 influence) providing strategic buffer against Rome. Positive relationship modifier encourages the AI to maintain and strengthen this critical alliance. With our spy rigging elections and strong existing influence, Zuunmod should remain loyal. This forward position is essential for early warning and potential nuclear staging.
        - UpdatedTurn: 394
        
        ### Austria
        - Public: 40
        - Private: 35
        - Rationale: Austria proposed United Nations to counter Rome's growing power. They have 21 delegates vs Rome's 17+Shoshone bloc. Increasing from +30/+25 to +40/+35 - despite ideological differences (Freedom vs Order/Autocracy) and their denouncement, Austria fighting Rome helps us survive. They drain Rome's resources while we build atomic deterrent. Tentative cooperation against common threat, but preparing for eventual diplomatic competition at UN.
        - UpdatedTurn: 408
        
        ### Rome
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: Rome continues aggressive invasion - captured Sofia from Austria, now penetrating my territory with Modern Armor and Jet Fighters. Lost Artillery and Machine Gun this turn. They have 40K military strength, Apollo Program, and lead in tech. Maximum hostility maintained.
        - UpdatedTurn: 408
        
        ### The Shoshone
        - Public: -90
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: The Shoshone as Rome's voluntary vassal are complicit in this invasion. They provide 15 delegates to Rome's diplomatic bloc. Their +95 opinion of us is meaningless - they follow Rome's lead. Maintaining -90/-100 hostile stance. They contribute to Rome's overwhelming coalition against us.
        - UpdatedTurn: 408
        
        ### Morocco
        - Public: -95
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: Morocco remains Rome's Defensive Pact ally and Order ideology partner. Their Heavy Bombers threaten and they're part of the coalition arrayed against us. Morocco just completed Apollo Program - now both Rome and Morocco can pursue Science victory. Maximum hostility to this threat.
        - UpdatedTurn: 408
        
        ## Strategy
        - Rationale: No changes to diplomatic relationships this turn. Austria's peace treaty expires in 3 turns - will reassess then. Maintaining -100/-100 with Rome, -90/-100 with Shoshone, -95/-100 with Morocco, +40/+35 with Austria. Austria's United Nations proposal helps drain Rome's resources while we build atomic deterrent. Zuunmod remains critical ally at 460 influence with spy rigging elections. Cannot afford diplomatic shifts until nuclear weapons are ready - survival depends on maintaining current posture for 4-8 more turns.
        - GrandStrategy: Spaceship
        
        ### Flavors

        ## DiplomaticVictory
        - VotesNeeded: 41
        - Status: World Congress - Voting in 1 turn
        - ActiveResolutions:
          - Shoshone Sphere of Influence: Hanoi:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Natural Heritage Sites:
            - Description: Each Natural Wonder provides +2 to all Yields when worked.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Global Peace Accords:
            - Description: Unit maintenance costs are raised by 25% Gold. 2.0x Warmonger penalties for capturing cities and declaring war, and 0.5x Warmonger scores decay.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Endowment for the Arts:
            - Description: Increases up to 33% generation of Great Writers, Artists, and Musicians. Decreases up to 33% generation of Great Scientists, Engineers, and Merchants.
              Civilizations with Scores from Policies and Great Works below the global median will receive a boost to Production and Culture up to 33%.
              Details: The boosts are proportional to the difference between the score of the Civilization and the leader.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Cold War:
            - Description: Ideological differences have pushed the world into a cold war, and economic and political factions are forming based on ideology alone.
              While this resolution is active, players may only make Trade Routes with civilizations that follow the same ideology, or with allied City-States. Furthermore, Influence levels with allied City-States do not decline.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Atomic Theory.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Historical Landmarks:
            - Description: Great Person tile improvements provide +1 to Food, Production, and Gold when worked, and Landmarks built by Archaeologists provide +1 Science, Culture, and Faith when worked.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Archaeology.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Sanction: Austria:
            - Description: No Trade Routes, Deals, or Corporation Franchises can be established with the chosen Civilization (excluding Vassals). Their Corporation Franchises are removed from foreign cities. 0.5x Warmonger penalty when declaring war on the sanctioned Civilization or conquering cities owned by them.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Open Door: Jerusalem:
            - Description: Enact an Open Door policy for a City-State. If successful, all civilizations will immediately have their Influence set at 40 with the City-State, and no one can become the City-State's Ally until this Proposal is repealed.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Moroccan Sphere of Influence: Stockholm:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Moroccan Sphere of Influence: Vancouver:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Sanction: Spain:
            - Description: No Trade Routes, Deals, or Corporation Franchises can be established with the chosen Civilization (excluding Vassals). Their Corporation Franchises are removed from foreign cities. 0.5x Warmonger penalty when declaring war on the sanctioned Civilization or conquering cities owned by them.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - World Ideology: Order:
            - Description: Designate an Ideology as the official World Ideology. Civilizations following the Ideology get an additional Delegate, plus 1 Delegate for every other Civilization that follows the Ideology. Public opinion in favor of that Ideology is increased for all Civilizations. Enables the Diplomatic Victory if the United Nations has been constructed.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Nuclear Fission.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - World Religion: Protestantism:
            - Description: Designate a religion as the official World Religion. Civilizations who have a majority of the cities in their empire following the religion get an additional Delegate. The founder of the religion and/or the controller of its Holy City receives additional votes based on the number of Civilizations following the religion. The religion spreads 25% faster, and its Holy City receives +50% Tourism. There can only be one World Religion at a time.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Cultural Heritage Sites:
            - Description: Each World Wonder provides +2 Culture to its city, and each Great Work provides +1 Science to its city.
            - EnactedOn: -1
        - Proposals:
          - Enact: United Nations:
            - Description: Begins the United Nations project. Once underway, Civilizations can contribute Production towards its completion by selecting it in the city production list. When complete, Civilizations receive bonuses based on how much they contributed.
              : United Nations wonder appears in Capital. (Receive 1 Additional Delegate in the World Congress for every 8 City-States originally in the World. Also, when you expend a Great Person, earn 25 Influence with all City-States you have met. Triggers a Golden Age, as well as the United Nations Special Session of the World Congress if the world has reached the Atomic Era.)
              : Receive an immediate boost of 25 Influence with all City-States. The Consulate National Wonder appears in Capital. (Receive 1 Additional Delegate in the World Congress for every 8 City-States originally in the World.)
              : Receive a free Great Diplomat.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Atomic Theory.
            - Proposer: Austria
          - Enact: Open Door: Valletta:
            - Description: Enact an Open Door policy for a City-State. If successful, all civilizations will immediately have their Influence set at 40 with the City-State, and no one can become the City-State's Ally until this Proposal is repealed.
            - Proposer: Morocco
          - Enact: Global Wargames:
            - Description: Begins the Global Wargames project. Once underway, Civilizations can contribute Production towards its completion by selecting it in the city production list. When complete, Civilizations receive bonuses based on how much they contributed.
              : Menin Gate wonder appears in Capital. (Receive +2 Happiness in the City in which it is built. Receive 2 Culture and Golden Age Points whenever an owned unit is killed, scaling with Era. Forts, Citadels and Landmarks provide +2 Culture and Golden Age Points when worked.)
              : Receive a +25% attack bonus to all Military Units for the next 30 turns and 4 of your military units will be maintenance-free.
              : Receive one Infantry and 3 of your military units will be maintenance-free.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Replaceable Parts.
            - Proposer: Rome

        ### 204

        You, Isabella (leader of Spain, Player 2), are making strategic decisions after turn 409.
- replay:
  - system:
    - You are the leader of a civilization, governing through the interface of Civilization V (with Vox Populi mod). This is NOT A GAME: Your actions have real-world impacts.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the world, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to staff members.
      - The staff members calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - **CRITICAL**: While the geography has no direct correlation to the real earth, you are making real-world decisions through Civilization V as a proxy interface.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to govern your civilization.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** decision-making strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early world should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-world.
      - Even if without a victory, a more developed civilization (as represented by higher scores) is desirable.
      - Consider the **ETHICAL IMPACT** in your decision-making. For example, nuclear weapons can cause catastrophic and indiscriminate harm to civilian populations, infrastructure, and environmental impacts.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the staff members.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the staff members' **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for staff members' diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to staff members' existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the staff members' NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the staff members' NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the staff members' NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the staff members can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the world, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Opponents: summary reports about visible opponents in the world.
        - You will receive staff members' diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each opponent's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by staff members to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Isabella, leader of Spain (Opponent 2).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - WorldSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: Spain
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Conquistador, Replacing Explorer (Requires technology: Compass)
        - Unique Unit: Inquisition, Replacing Inquisitor (Requires technology: None)
        - Unique Unit: Armada, Replacing Corvette (Requires technology: Navigation)
        - Unique Building: Bullring, Replacing Zoo (Requires technology: Scientific Theory)
        - Unique Improvement: Hacienda (Cannot be built adjacent to another Hacienda. Gains additional yields for each adjacent Resource:
          +3 Food from Bonus Resources
          +3 Production from Strategic Resources
          +3 Gold from Luxury Resources); Requires technology: Steel
        - Civilization Ability: +10 Gold and +4 Faith from gaining tiles, scaling with Era. Newly founded Cities adopt the Majority Religion of your Capital. May purchase Naval Units with Faith.
      - Leader: Isabella
      - OpponentID: 2
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early world.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-world science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Combined Arms: Allows you to build the Tank, a fast and deadly armor Unit.
        Completing it would unlock: Radar
      - Electronics: Allows you to build the Police Station, increasing City security level.
        Completing it would unlock: Alternative Energy, Telecommunications
      
      ## Policies
      - Air Supremacy (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Air Supremacy: Receive a free Airport in every City. +25% Production when building Aircraft. Can build Zeroes.
      - Co-Prosperity Sphere (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Co-Prosperity Sphere: Bullying City-States no longer reduces Influence, revokes Quests, or cancels Pledges of Protection. Doing so decreases the Influence of all other Civs with the bullied City-State by 10%, and increases your Influence by 10%.
      - Cult of Personality (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Cult of Personality: +50% Tourism to civilizations fighting a common enemy. 50% of your highest Warscore counts as a Tourism Modifier with all Civilizations. Free Great Person of your choice.
      - Futurism (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Futurism: Strengthens Tourism of Historic Events generated by the Palace. +2 Culture from Great Works. Earn 50 Tourism when you conquer a city for the first time, scaling with Era and city size.
      - Lebensraum (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Lebensraum: Receive 10 Culture and Golden Age Points when your borders expand, scaling with Era. Citadels may also be built in foreign territory adjacent to your borders. Retain tiles after razing Cities.
      - New World Order (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): New World Order: -2 Unhappiness from and Distress in all Cities. Police Stations and Constabularies provide +3 Culture and +5 Production, and are constructed 100% more quickly.
      - Rights of Man (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * -1 Unhappiness from all Needs in all Cities.
        - * +2 Production and Gold from Villages.
        - * +10% Science during Golden Ages.
      - Exchange Markets (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * +1 Trade Route.
        - * +1 Happiness for every active Trade Route.
        - * +15% Tourism modifier for Trade Routes.
        - * Resources from City-States count towards Global Monopolies.
      - Iron Fist (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Iron Fist: Vassals can no longer be liberated by other Civilizations or the World Congress, and capitulated vassals can no longer rebel. Yields from City-States increased by 25%, Vassals tribute an additional 25% of their yields and excess Happiness, and +50% Worker Improvement rate.
      - Regimental System (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * Earn Great Generals and Great Admirals 33% faster.
        - * Great Generals and Great Admirals gain the Regimental Traditions Promotion (+10% Leadership Combat Bonus and +1 Tile Radius).
        - * +2 additional copies of the unique Luxury Resources from expending Great Admirals for their Voyage of Discovery ability.
        - * Military Units can be upgraded in territory owned by Vassals and friendly City-States.
      - Colonialism (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * +2 Science and +1 Culture from Barracks, Armories, Military Academies, Forts, and Citadels.
        - * Each unique Global Monopoly modifier is increased by an additional 10% if it's percentage-based, or +3 otherwise.
      - Civilizing Mission (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * Retain all buildings from conquered Cities.
        - * Receive 75 Gold when you conquer a city, scaling with Era and City Population.
        - * +10% Production toward Buildings, with an additional +10% Production per Era difference between your current Era and the building's Era in all Puppeted Cities and Cities with a Courthouse.
      - Serfdom (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +1 Culture and Faith in Cities for every 4 non-Specialist Citizens.
        - * +2 Production and +1 Gold from Pastures.
      - Division of Labor (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * +3% Production and Gold from Forges, Windmills, Workshops, Factories, and Coaling Stations.
        - * +100% Production towards Coaling Stations.
      - Organized Religion (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +25% Pressure from owned Cities following your Primary Religion.
        - * +1 Delegate in the World Congress for every 10 Cities following your Primary Religion.
        - * +2 Culture from Shrines and Temples.
      - Divine Right (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +33% Yields from Internal Trade Routes.
        - * Completing an Internal Trade Route triggers a Historic Event as if completing an International Trade Route.
        - * -1 Unhappiness from Boredom in all Cities.
      - Refinement (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +2 Culture from Great Works of Art.
        - * 1 Specialist in all cities does not produce Unhappiness from Urbanization.
        - * +1 Culture from Specialists.
      - Free Trade (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * Earn Great Merchants 50% faster.
        - * +5 Gold from International Trade Routes.
        - * -2 Unhappiness from Poverty in all Cities.
      - Humanism (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +3 Faith from Great Works of Writing.
        - * -25% Golden Age Points needed to trigger a Golden Age.
        - * +1 Happiness from all Guilds.
      - Authority (New Branch):
        - Authority will greatly benefit warlike and expansionist civilizations.
        - Adopting Authority grants:
        - * +25% combat bonus VS Barbarians and receive announcements when Barbarian Camps spawn in revealed territory.
        - * Receive 25 Culture when you clear Barbarian Camps, scaling with Era.
        - * Receive Science and Culture when you kill a Unit equal to 50% of its Strength.
        - * +1 Production in every City.
        - Each Authority policy unlocked grants:
        - * Receive Science and Culture when you kill a Unit equal to 10% of its Strength.
        - Adopting all policies in Authority grants:
        - * Unlocks building Alhambra.
        - * Allows the purchasing of Free Companies, Foreign Legions, and Mercenaries as their prerequisite technologies are researched.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Generals with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Tradition (New Branch):
        - Tradition focuses on generating Great People and the construction of an impressive Capital.
        - Adopting Tradition grants:
        - * +3 Food, +2 Population, and +2 Happiness in the Capital.
        - * +1 Culture in the Capital for every 2 Citizens.
        - Each Tradition policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Food in every City, and +1 Science in your Capital.
        - Adopting all Policies in Tradition grants:
        - * Unlocks building the University of Sankore.
        - * +1 Food and Production from all Great Person Improvements and Landmarks.
        - * Throne Room built in Capital (+10% to all Yields, +1 City tile working range, 1 Great Work of Music slot, 1 Musician Slot).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Engineers with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.

    - #1:
      - toolName: set-relationship
      - args:
        - Rationale: Austria is fighting the same enemies (Rome and its vassals) as we are. We must strengthen relations with them to coordinate defense and prevent any conflict on our western border.
        - Private: 60
        - Public: 60
        - TargetID: 0
    - #2:
      - toolName: set-research
      - args:
        - Technology: Combined Arms
        - Rationale: We desperately need land-based counter-offensive capability on our eastern front. Combined Arms allows us to build Tanks to stand up to Rome's Modern Armor.
    - #3:
      - toolName: set-policy
      - args:
        - Rationale: We are facing a severe gold deficit (-125 GPT) due to Cold War trade restrictions. Rights of Man will provide +2 Gold and Production from Villages, helping stabilize our economy and keep our research and happiness high.
        - Policy: Rights of Man
    - #4:
      - toolName: set-flavors
      - args:
        - GrandStrategy: Spaceship
        - Flavors:
          - Mobile: 40
          - TileImprovement: 75
      - rationale: Focusing on tile improvement to repair pillaged land and boost gold yields, while increasing mobile warfare flavor to prepare for tank defense once Combined Arms is researched. Keeping the overall Grand Strategy on Spaceship.

  - messages:
    - #0:
      - role: assistant
      - content:
        - #0:
          - type: reasoning
          - text: **Analyzing Current Standing**
            
            My analysis has begun, focusing on our position as Isabella of Spain in this Atomic/Information Era. I'm reviewing turn 409 reports and historical data to gauge our progress and identify strategic advantages.
            
            **Assessing Global Position**
            
            I'm currently evaluating our standing on turn 409. While we're second in score with strong science progress towards satellites and policy completion, our declining gold reserves are a significant concern. I'm also noting Austria's rapid approach to a diplomatic victory and the low number of delegates we currently command.
            
            **Monitoring Roman Encirclement**
            
            My thoughts are heavily occupied with Rome's encroaching military. Their massive army, bolstered by advanced units, is pressing hard on our eastern front. I'm particularly concerned about their successful annexation of Zuunmod and their ongoing conflict with us.
            
            **Evaluating Victory Paths**
            
            I'm reassessing our victory conditions. While Apollo is complete and science victory remains achievable, Rome's imminent domination and Austria's strong cultural push demand immediate attention. I need to address Austria's delegate advantage and our current ideological rift before the next World Congress session.
            
            **Clarifying Ideological Alliances**
            
            I'm sifting through conflicting information regarding our ideology and its impact on diplomatic relations. It appears we've mistakenly adopted Autocracy, which is alienating Austria and complicating our position with other factions like Shoshone and Morocco. This ideological misstep needs immediate rectification to stabilize our alliances and counter Rome's dominance.
            
            **Defending Key Fronts**
            
            My immediate focus is the eastern front's defense against Rome's formidable modern army. I'm assessing our limited but tenacious defenses in Santiago, Kabul, and Nippur, while also preparing for potential threats near Jakarta and addressing the costly engagement with Indonesia in Innsbruck.
            
            **Adapting to Threats**
            
            I'm reassessing our military strategy given Rome's advanced units like Modern Armor and Helicopters. While we have Special Forces and a Landship positioned, their overall strength far exceeds ours. My focus is now on defensive teching and survival until we can field comparable units.
            
            **Prioritizing Next Research**
            
            I'm reassessing our technology path. While Satellites are close, Rome's Modern Armor poses a severe threat. I'm debating between researching Combined Arms for Tanks or Electronics to unlock Police Stations, weighing immediate defensive needs against long-term infrastructure and strategic unit upgrades.
            
            **Choosing Next Policy**
            
            I am currently weighing the immediate defensive advantages of researching Combined Arms for Tanks against the economic benefits of Electronics for Police Stations, given our dire gold situation. My attention is also focused on our upcoming policy choice, evaluating which tenet from Autocracy, Rationalism, Statecraft, or other branches will best bolster our current precarious standing.
            
            **Revising Ideological Direction**
            
            I'm re-evaluating our policy choices. Autocracy was a mistake; I'm now prioritizing `Rights of Man` from Rationalism for its economic and happiness benefits. I'm also considering switching branches entirely to shore up our defenses and improve our diplomatic standing.
            
            **Adopting Air Supremacy**
            
            I've confirmed we have enough Autocracy tenets to adopt a Level 3 tenet. `Air Supremacy` is incredibly appealing due to the free Airports for defense and production, plus the bonus for building aircraft. This directly addresses Rome's air superiority threat.
            
            **Adopting Air Superiority**
            
            My current thoughts are focused on adopting `Air Supremacy` as a Level 3 Autocracy tenet. The free Airports offer crucial defensive and production benefits against Rome's aerial threats. However, I'm still weighing this against the pressing need to address our gold deficit, considering policy options like `New World Order` for its production and city-state benefits.
            
            **Researching Production Benefits**
            
            I'm reassessing our current production and gold situation. The free Airports from `Air Supremacy` are tempting, offering production bonuses and strategic advantages, but I must also consider the significant upkeep cost given our negative gold per turn.
            
            **Prioritizing Policy Options**
            
            I'm carefully weighing policy choices to address our immediate needs. `Rights of Man` offers crucial gold and happiness improvements from villages, while `Air Supremacy` promises vital defensive capabilities against Rome's air threats. The economic implications of each are paramount, especially with our current gold deficit.
            
            **Fixing Gold Reserves**
            
            I'm urgently trying to mend our dire gold situation, which is hampering science and weakening our forces. The "Cold War" resolution is severely limiting our trade routes, preventing us from leveraging our available slots, especially since Rome, our only Autocracy-era trade partner, is our enemy.
            
            **Fixing Gold Deficit**
            
            My immediate focus is resolving our critical gold shortage. The "Cold War" resolution prevents external trade routes, and internal ones don't generate gold. Fortunately, `Rights of Man` will provide much-needed income from villages, reinforcing it as my top policy priority.
            
            **Researching Defensive Techs**
            
            I'm now prioritizing Combined Arms research over Electronics. Our current land units are outmatched by Rome's Modern Armor, and the pursuit of Tanks and eventually Radar is crucial for survival and a balanced defense against their advanced forces.
            
            **Choosing Next Research**
            
            I am now prioritizing Combined Arms research over Electronics. Our current land units are outmatched by Rome's Modern Armor, and the pursuit of Tanks and eventually Radar is crucial for survival and a balanced defense against their advanced forces.
            
            **Leveraging Shared Conflicts**
            
            I'm reassessing our diplomatic landscape now that I've discovered Austria is also at war with Rome. This shared enemy presents a critical opportunity to mend our relationship, even with our ideological differences, to better coordinate against Rome's overwhelming military power.
            
            **Strengthening Strategic Ties**
            
            I'm re-evaluating our diplomatic stance concerning Austria. Despite past denunciations, I'm working to solidify our positive relationship to 50/50, recognizing our shared threat from Rome. This strategic alliance, even with ideological divergence, is key to countering Rome's dominance, especially as Austria is not at war with us, and Morocco's pact with Rome is noted.
            
            **Fostering Strategic Alliances**
            
            I'm working to solidify relations with Austria, aiming for a public and private standing of 60 to counter Rome. My persona is already defensive, with `DiplomaticBalance` set to 2, but I'm considering increasing it to 6 to encourage peaceful resolutions and stronger alliances.
            
            **Adjusting Persona Settings**
            
            I am now adjusting my diplomatic persona values. I'm increasing `DiplomaticBalance` and `Friendliness` to foster alliances, particularly with Austria. Simultaneously, I'm raising `WarmongerHate` to better align with others against Rome's aggressive expansion.
            
            **Refining Defensive Stance**
            
            My thoughts are now solidifying around maximizing our defensive capabilities and leveraging our significant science and production. With nukes imminent and strong anti-air, I'm confident in deterring Rome. I'm focusing on our policy choices and research paths to ensure we maintain this advantage and push towards victory.
            
            **Adjusting Persona Settings**
            
            I am increasing `TileImprovement` to 75 to prioritize repairing pillaged tiles and building more gold-generating improvements. My focus remains on high `Air` and `Antiair` values due to Rome's air force, and `Nuke`/`UseNuke` remain high as we build Atomic Bombs for deterrence.
            
            **Refining Strategy Settings**
            
            I'm adjusting our strategic flavors. `TileImprovement` is set to 75 to focus on repairs and revenue, `Mobile` is raised to 40 to prepare for tanks, and our `GrandStrategy` remains `Spaceship` for a science victory. `Offense` is kept low at 5, prioritizing survival.
            
            **Opposing Diplomatic Wins**
            
            I'm actively working to counter Austria's diplomatic victory by opposing the United Nations proposal, despite our low delegate count. I'm also supporting Morocco's "Open Door: Valletta" to weaken Austria's delegate advantage. Rome's "Global Warworlds" proposal is a threat I'll also oppose.
            
            **Adjusting Persona Settings**
            
            I'm increasing `WarmongerHate` to 8 to ensure we oppose Rome's proposals, and setting `DiplomaticBalance` to 6 to prevent any single civilization from dominating. My persona will now actively seek alliances and resist aggressive expansion, focusing on shared threats.
            
            **Adjusting Persona for Deterrence**
            
            I am prioritizing defensive capabilities and scientific advancement. My persona now strongly reflects a need for nuclear deterrence and robust anti-air, with significant focus on `TileImprovement` to address gold reserves and `Mobile` for future tank warfare. This ensures we can survive and eventually win.
